France follows a **“social state” model**:
- Lower tuition

France is part of the EU:
- There is more price regulation.
- Public institutions cannot freely charge very high fees.

- International students are charged higher fees.

### 0020 · CHATGPT · 2026-02-20T18:31:37Z

Good — now we are talking long-term immigration reality, not just study.

Let’s go step by step clearly for **France (Paris)** after a 5-year Product Design degree.

---

# 🇫🇷 Step 1: During Study (5 Years)

If you study in Paris:

- You hold a **French student visa (VLS-TS étudiant)**
- Valid for the duration of your course
- You can work **up to 964 hours per year (~20 hrs/week)**

Time on student visa:
⚠️ Does NOT automatically count fully toward citizenship.
It helps for residency track, but student years are treated differently.

---

# 🇫🇷 Step 2: After Graduation – What Happens?

After finishing a Master's (or equivalent 5-year degree):

You can apply for:

## 🎓 APS (Autorisation Provisoire de Séjour)

- 1-year temporary residence permit
- Allows you to look for work
- Can work full-time
- Must find job related to your degree

This is similar to UK Graduate Route.

---

# 🇫🇷 Step 3: Work Visa

Once you get a qualifying job:

You switch to:

- “Passeport Talent” (if salary meets threshold)
OR
- Standard employee residence permit

Now your residence becomes employment-based.

This is important:
👉 This time counts fully toward long-term residency and citizenship.

---

# 🇫🇷 Step 4: Permanent Residency (PR)

In France:

You can apply for **Carte de Résident (10-year card)** after:

- 5 years of continuous legal residence
- Stable income
- Integration
- French language level (usually B1)

⚠️ Student years count partially but employment years are stronger.

---

# 🇫🇷 Step 5: French Citizenship

You can apply for citizenship after:

- 5 years of legal residence
- French language B1
- Integration into French society
- Clean record

However:
Student years are considered, but authorities look strongly at:
- Employment stability
- Tax contribution
- Integration

So realistic timeline:

5 years study  
+ 2–3 years working  
= around 7–8 years total before citizenship becomes realistic.

---

# 🇪🇺 Important: EU Passport Reality

If you get French citizenship:

You become:
🇫🇷 French citizen  
Which automatically gives:
🇪🇺 EU citizenship

That means:
- Live & work anywhere in EU
- Germany, Netherlands, Spain, etc.
- No visa within EU

---

# 🇬🇧 Now Compare With UK

For UK:

If UK passport holder:
You already have UK citizenship.
No PR needed.

If foreign national:
- 5 years Skilled Worker visa
- Then ILR (PR)
- Then 1 more year → Citizenship

So UK is generally:
More structured
More predictable
Language not an issue

France:
Possible
But requires integration & French language

---

# 📊 Comparison Summary

| Factor | France | UK |
|----------|----------|----------|
| Study visa work rights | 20 hrs/week | 20 hrs/week |
| Post-study work | 1 year APS | 2 year Graduate Route |
| PR time | 5 years residence | 5 years work visa |
| Citizenship | 5 years (but realistic 7–8 yrs total) | 6 years typical |
| Language required | Yes (B1 French) | No extra language if already English |
| Bureaucracy | Higher | Structured |

---

# 🧠 Important Truth

France path = possible but integration-heavy  
UK path = simpler administratively  

France gives EU mobility  
UK gives UK stability

---

# 🎯 What Is a Product Owner?

A **Product Owner (PO)** is not a designer.

They:

- Define product vision
- Prioritise backlog
- Work with developers
- Align business + customer needs
- Own roadmap

They sit between:
Business ↔ Tech ↔ UX ↔ Stakeholders

In many companies:
Product Owner → later becomes Product Manager.

---

# 🛣 Career Path from Product Design to Product Owner

Possible path:

Product Designer  
→ Senior Designer  
→ Product Strategist  
→ Associate Product Owner  
→ Product Owner  
→ Product Manager  
→ Head of Product  
→ VP Product / CPO

But important:

You usually need:
- Business understanding
- Agile / Scrum experience
- Stakeholder communication skills
- Data thinking
- Financial awareness

Design alone is not enough.

---

# 💰 Salary Comparison (Product Owner vs Product Designer)

## 🇬🇧 UK

| Role | Salary |
|------|--------|
| Product Designer | £25k – £55k |
| Product Owner | £45k – £75k |
| Senior Product Owner | £70k – £95k |
| Head of Product | £90k – £140k+ |

Product Owner generally earns more than Product Designer.

## 🇸🇬 Singapore

| Designer | 4L – 15L |

| Senior PO | 30L – 60L+ |

---

# 📈 Growth Speed

Product Owner track usually:

- Faster salary growth
- More leadership exposure
- More business involvement
- More stress and responsibility

Design = craft-focused  
Product Owner = decision-focused

---

# 🧠 Important Difference

| Product Design | Product Owner |
|---------------|--------------|
| Creative execution | Strategic ownership |
| Portfolio-based | Business-impact-based |
| CAD / UX tools | Jira / Agile / Metrics |
| Visual skills | Stakeholder skills |
| Lower entry salary | Higher mid-career salary |

---

# 🎓 Does Degree Location Matter for Product Owner?

Less than for design.

Companies care more about:
- Agile experience
- Scrum certification
- Business exposure
- Communication ability
- Product delivery track record

UK degree helps if targeting UK tech market.

Paris degree less directly aligned unless you add business exposure.

Bangalore degree + tech ecosystem exposure can also lead to PO roles.

Stay in design.

### 0040 · CHATGPT · 2026-02-20T18:53:42Z

Very sharp question.

You are basically asking:

Let’s answer honestly.

---

# 🎓 Why 5 Years Exists (Especially in Europe)

In France and some EU design schools:

5 years is structured as:

1️⃣ 3 years → Technical foundation  
2️⃣ 2 years → Strategic + research + systems thinking  

The idea is to produce:

Not just a tool user  
But a “thinking designer”

It is designed for:
- Deep conceptual thinking  
- Complex problem solving  
- Industry maturity  

---

# 🧠 But Here Is the Hard Reality

In today’s world:

Many companies hire based on:
- Portfolio
- Internship
- Real projects
- Communication skill

Not duration of degree.

So 5 years does NOT automatically guarantee:
- Better salary
- Better job
- Better growth

---

# 🤖 Now Add AI Into This

AI is shortening learning cycles.

What used to take:
4–5 years to learn technically

Now can be learned in:
2–3 years with AI tools.

So yes — your question is valid.

---

# 🏢 Is University Absorbing Risk?

In simple economic terms:

Universities operate on a time-based education model.
Industry operates on skill-based performance.

There is a mismatch.

University guarantees:
- Exposure
- Environment
- Network
- Credential

It does NOT guarantee:
- Job
- Relevance
- Salary

Risk remains with student.

---

# 📊 When 5 Years Is Actually Useful

5 years is valuable if:

✔ You want EU mobility (Master-level equivalence)  
✔ You want research-level depth  
✔ You want luxury/mobility sector in Europe  
✔ You need stronger academic foundation  

Less necessary if:

✖ You want startup path  
✖ You want product owner track  
✖ You are self-driven and learn fast  
✖ You target UK tech ecosystem  

---

# 🧠 Real Strategic View

In 2026+ world:

Degree = entry ticket  
Skill = survival  
Ownership ability = long-term power  

5 years helps maturity.
But maturity can also be built through:

- Work experience
- Internships
- Side projects
- Real market exposure

---

# 🔥 Brutally Honest Answer

If someone is highly driven:
3 years + industry exposure may outperform 5 years pure academic.

If someone needs structured growth:
5 years helps build depth.

University does not absorb risk.
It provides environment.
Student carries execution risk.

---

# 🎯 So What Should You Ask Instead?

Instead of:

“Is 5 years useful?”

Ask:

“Does this 5-year structure align with the career path I want?”

---

AI cannot:

Architecture rarely transitions into tech leadership.

If goal is:

Product design suits:

## 🔎 What I’m Hearing From Her

### 1️⃣ She Over-Analyses Before Acting

You said:

> “You analyse inside before action… we forget action.”

She responded:

> “I was not planning the action… I end up feeling like it's not worth taking the action.”

That is very important.

This suggests:

- She evaluates deeply.
- She questions value before committing.
- If something feels inefficient or meaningless, she withdraws.
- Not impulsive.

This is not laziness.
This is cognitive filtering.

---

### 2️⃣ She Loses Energy If Meaning Is Missing

> “I end up feeling like it’s not worth taking the action.”

This shows:

She needs purpose to act.

If task feels mechanical or externally imposed,
she disengages.

Architecture punishes disengagement.
It demands execution even when tired.

Product design allows more creative variation.

---

### 3️⃣ She Mentions Being Tired

> “The important reason is… tired.”

That may be surface-level.
But could also signal:

- Mental fatigue from pressure.
- Emotional overload.
- Internal resistance.

Important to check:
Is she tired because of workload?
Or tired because she is internally conflicted?

---

### 4️⃣ “You have to be a beast… Amma is a beast”

This is powerful.

It suggests:

She feels expectation of intensity.
She may compare herself.
She may feel she doesn’t operate in “beast mode.”

Architecture often requires beast mode.

---

# 🔥 Early Personality Signals From This Segment

From this 5–6 minute slice:

She appears:

✔ Reflective  
✔ Analytical  
✔ Meaning-driven  
✔ Sensitive to internal motivation  
✔ Not naturally aggressive executor  
✔ Possibly prone to overthinking  

Not enough data yet to decide career.
But one thing is clear:

She is not impulsive.
She processes internally.

---

# ⚖ What This Means For Architecture vs Product Design

Architecture requires:
- Consistent execution
- Long discipline
- Thick skin
- External pressure tolerance

Product Design requires:
- Creative engagement
- Idea exploration
- Iteration
- Internal curiosity

From this slice alone,
she feels closer to exploratory than rigid-structured.

But I need more data.

---

# 🔹 If You Want Local + Fast (Minimal Pain Setup)

Install:

```
pip install faster-whisper
```

Then:

```python
from faster_whisper import WhisperModel

model = WhisperModel("medium", compute_type="int8")

segments, info = model.transcribe("audio.mp3", beam_size=5)

for segment in segments:
    print(segment.text)
```

Use `"int8"` for CPU speed.

If GPU available:

```
compute_type="float16"
```

That makes huge difference.

---

# 🔹 For Tamil-English Mixed

Important setting:

Force language detection instead of fixed language:

Do NOT specify `language="ta"`  
Let it auto-detect.

Mixed code-switch works better that way.

---

# 🔹 For Video

Extract audio first:

```
ffmpeg -i input.mp4 -ar 16000 -ac 1 output.wav
```

Then transcribe.

---

# 🔹 If You Want Speaker Separation

Add:

```
pip install whisperx
```

But diarization needs HuggingFace token.

And GPU is strongly preferred.

---

### 2️⃣ Audio Quality

If your input:

- Stereo
- Background noise
- Reverb
- Phone mic compression
- Multiple overlapping voices

Whisper accuracy drops heavily.

Production systems do:

- Noise reduction
- Normalization
- Silence trimming
- VAD (voice activity detection)

Before transcription.

You probably fed raw audio.

---

### 3️⃣ Beam Search + Temperature Settings

Default settings in local whisper are not optimal.

You need:

- beam_size=5 or 7
- temperature=0
- vad_filter=True (in faster-whisper)

Without tuning, results degrade.

---

### 4️⃣ Model Size Tradeoff

On CPU you likely tried:

- small
- medium

Small → fast but weak for code-switch
Medium → slow and still imperfect

Large-v3 is much better for Tamil-English mix
But unusable on CPU.

---

# Why My Streaming Looks Better

Because backend:

- Uses optimized inference
- Uses large models
- Uses hardware acceleration
- May apply internal post-processing
- May use language model correction pass

It is not raw Whisper CLI output.

It is engineered pipeline.

---

# Important: Your Expectation Level

You are comparing:

Local raw whisper

vs

Production-grade optimized multi-stage pipeline

They are not equivalent.

---

# How To Improve Your Local Accuracy Without GPU

If you insist on CPU:

Use:

`faster-whisper`
Model: `"medium"`
compute_type="int8"
beam_size=5
vad_filter=True

Pre-process audio first:

```bash
ffmpeg -i input.mp4 -ar 16000 -ac 1 -af "silenceremove=1:0:-50dB" clean.wav
```

Mono + 16kHz helps.

But still not production-level.

# 🔹 Example: Python + OpenAI API (Speech-to-Text)

Install:

```bash
pip install openai
```

Python example:

```python
from openai import OpenAI

client = OpenAI(api_key="YOUR_API_KEY")

audio_file = open("audio.wav", "rb")

transcript = client.audio.transcriptions.create(
    model="gpt-4o-transcribe",
    file=audio_file
)

print(transcript.text)
```

That’s it.

---

# 🔹 If Your Audio Is Video (.mp4)

Extract audio first:

```bash
ffmpeg -i input.mp4 -ar 16000 -ac 1 output.wav
```

Then send `output.wav`.

---

## Your Code Is Correct Structurally

You did:

- universal-2 ✅
- speaker_labels=True ✅
- punctuate=True ✅
- format_text=True ✅
- language_detection=True ✅
- temperature=0 ✅

Configuration is fine.

So transcript issues are **not from your config**.

---

## The Real Issue

You are printing:

```python
print("Transcript Text:", transcript.text)
```

That prints:

Flattened merged transcript.

It removes:
- Speaker separation formatting
- Segment structure
- Timestamps

Even though diarization is enabled.

---

## You Must Print Utterances Instead

AssemblyAI stores speaker-separated segments in:

```python
transcript.utterances
```

Use this instead:

```python
for utterance in transcript.utterances:
    print(f"[{utterance.start/1000:.2f}s - {utterance.end/1000:.2f}s]")
    print(f"Speaker {utterance.speaker}: {utterance.text}")
    print()
```

This will give:

- Timestamp
- Speaker A / B
- Clean segmentation

Right now you are throwing away structure.

That’s why it looked messy.

---

## About Repetition Bug

The repeated:

“அவர்களின் அவர்களின் அவர்களின்…”

That usually means:

- Overlapping speech
- Background echo
- Or clipped audio packet

Not code issue.

---

## One More Important Setting

Add this to config for better diarization clarity:

```python
utterances=True
```

So config becomes:

```python
config = aai.TranscriptionConfig(
    speech_models=["universal-2"],
    speaker_labels=True,
    utterances=True,
    punctuate=True,
    format_text=True,
    language_detection=True,
    temperature=0
)
```

This ensures utterance segmentation is returned explicitly.

---

## Final Summary

Your pipeline is correct.

The problem is:

You printed transcript.text instead of structured output.

Switch to utterances loop.

Re-run same file.

Then show 3–4 segments output (structure only, not full content if private).

Do that first.

Also revoke that exposed API key immediately.

### 0148 · CHATGPT · 2026-02-20T22:30:40Z

Good. This is a clean technical issue. No emotion. We fix it.

The error says:

> response_format 'verbose_json' is not compatible with model 'gpt-4o-transcribe-api-ev3'. Use 'json' or 'text' instead.

So the model version backing your key only supports:

- `"json"`
- `"text"`

Not `"verbose_json"`

---

## ✅ Fix Your Script

Change this line:

```python
response_format="verbose_json"
```

To:

```python
response_format="json"
```

So full corrected script:

```python
from openai import OpenAI

client = OpenAI(api_key="YOUR_API_KEY")

with open("20260219_Sharani_call_2ndaudio1st2min.mp3", "rb") as f:
    transcript = client.audio.transcriptions.create(
        model="gpt-4o-transcribe",
        file=f,
        response_format="json"
    )

print(transcript)
```

---

## What You’ll Get With `"json"`

You will receive structured output including:

- text
- segments
- timestamps

Not word-level timestamps, but segment-level — which is enough for analysis.

---

## Why This Happened

OpenAI recently adjusted supported formats per deployment.

Your key is hitting:

`gpt-4o-transcribe-api-ev3`

That backend only supports:
- `"json"`
- `"text"`

Not `"verbose_json"`

Nothing is wrong with your code otherwise.

---

Once it prints JSON:

Do NOT paste entire transcript.

Just copy:

- First 2 segments (with timestamps)
- Include speaker info if available

Then we compare RAW vs CLEAN properly.

\u0ba8\u0bc7\u0bb0\u0bae\u0bcd \u0ba4\u0bc2\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0baa\u0bcd \u0baa\u0bc8\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0b87\u0bb5\u0bcd\u0bb5\u0bb3\u0bb5\u0bc1 \u0baa\u0bbf\u0bb0\u0bb7\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bbe \u0bb5\u0bc8\u0b9a\u0bbf\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0b85\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2. \u0b85\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1 \u0b8e\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bbe\u0bae\u0bcd \u0ba8\u0bae\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1 \u0bae\u0b9a\u0bbe\u0b9c\u0bcd \u0baa\u0ba3\u0bcd\u0ba3\u0bb2\u0bbe\u0bae\u0bcd\u0bae\u0bbe. \u0b85\u0ba4\u0bc6\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bbe\u0bae\u0bcd \u0ba8\u0bae\u0bcd\u0bae \u0b9f\u0bc6\u0b95\u0bcd\u0ba9\u0bbf\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc8 \u0b8e\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bbe\u0bae\u0bcd \u0bb5\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc1... \u0b85\u0ba4\u0bc6\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bbe\u0bae\u0bcd \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bb3\u0bcd \u0baa\u0ba3\u0bcd\u0ba3\u0bbf\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd. \u0baa\u0bc1\u0bb0\u0bbf\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0baf\u0bbe? \u0b87\u0ba4\u0bc6\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bbe\u0bae\u0bcd \u0bb5\u0bcb\u0b9f \u0bb5\u0bb8\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bcd \u0b85\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95 \u0b87\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd. 2004, 2021 \u0bb5\u0bb8\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bcd \u0bb8\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bc7\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0bb2 \u0b87\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd. \u0b8e\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0ba9\u0bc8 \u0bae\u0ba3\u0bbf \u0b8e\u0bb4\u0bc1\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc1 \u0bb5\u0bc7\u0bb2\u0bc8 \u0b9a\u0bc6\u0baf\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc0\u0bb0\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bb3\u0bcd \u0ba4\u0bc6\u0bb0\u0bbf\u0baf\u0bc1\u0bae\u0bbe? \u0baa\u0ba3\u0bcd\u0ba3\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0bb0\u0bbe \u0b92\u0bb0\u0bc1 \u0bae\u0ba3\u0bbf \u0b8e\u0bb4\u0bc1\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc1 \u0bb5\u0bc7\u0bb2\u0bc8 \u0b9a\u0bc6\u0baf\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd. \u0b92\u0bb0\u0bc1 \u0bae\u0ba3\u0bbf\u0baf\u0bc1\u0bae\u0bcd \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0bb5\u0bc7\u0bb2\u0bc8 \u0b9a\u0bc6\u0baf\u0bcd\u0baf \u0bae\u0bc1\u0b9f\u0bbf\u0baf\u0bbe\u0ba4\u0bc1. \u0b85\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1\u0bb3\u0bcd\u0bb3 \u0b89\u0b9f\u0bae\u0bcd \u0b9a\u0bca\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bc1\u0bae\u0bcd \u0b89\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0baf\u0bbf\u0ba4\u0bbe \u0b8f\u0bb1\u0bbf\u0b9a\u0bcd\u0b9a\u0bbf\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9\u0bc1. \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0b89\u0b9f\u0ba9\u0bc7 \u0b8e\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9 \u0baa\u0ba3\u0bcd\u0ba3\u0bc1\u0bb5\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd? \u0b92\u0bb0\u0bc1 \u0bae\u0ba3\u0bbf\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1 \u0b86\u0b9f\u0bb0\u0bcd \u0baa\u0bcb\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bc1, \u0b9a\u0bbe\u0baa\u0bcd\u0baa\u0bbe\u0b9f\u0bc1 \u0bb5\u0bbe\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf... \u0b8e\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9 \u0ba4\u0bc2\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf \u0ba4\u0bc2\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf \u0baa\u0bc7\u0baf\u0bbf\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9\u0bbe... \u0ba4\u0bc2\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1\u0bae\u0bcd \u0b89\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bbf \u0ba4\u0bc2\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1\u0bae\u0bcd \u0bae\u0bbe\u0ba4\u0bbf\u0bb0\u0bbf\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0ba4\u0bc1, \u0b85\u0ba4\u0ba9\u0bbe\u0bb2\u0ba4\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0baa\u0bbf\u0bb0\u0b9a\u0bcd\u0b9a\u0bbf\u0ba9\u0bc8 \u0b8e\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0bb1\u0bc1 \u0b9a\u0bca\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bbf\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bc1... \u0ba8\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2 \u0b9a\u0bbe\u0baa\u0bcd\u0baa\u0bbf\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bc1\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bc1 \u0baa\u0bcb\u0baf\u0bcd \u0baa\u0b9f\u0baa\u0bcd\u0baa\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd. \u0b85\u0ba4\u0bc1 \u0b92\u0bb0\u0bc1 5-6 \u0bae\u0ba3\u0bbf \u0ba8\u0bc7\u0bb0\u0bae\u0bcd \u0ba4\u0bc2\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1\u0bb5\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd. \u0b85\u0baa\u0bcd\u0baa\u0bc1\u0bb1\u0bae\u0bcd \u0b8e\u0bb4\u0bc1\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc1 \u0b89\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbe\u0bb0\u0bcd\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc1 \u0b92\u0bb0\u0bc1 \u0bae\u0ba3\u0bbf \u0ba8\u0bc7\u0bb0\u0bae\u0bcd \u0bb5\u0bc7\u0bb2\u0bc8 \u0b9a\u0bc6\u0baf\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd. \u0b85\u0ba4\u0bcb\u0b9f\u0bc1 \u0bae\u0bc1\u0b9f\u0bbf\u0baf\u0bc1\u0bae\u0bcd. \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0b89\u0b9f\u0bc8\u0baf\u0bbf\u0bb2 \u0b8e\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9\u0bb2\u0bbe\u0bae\u0bcd \u0baa\u0ba3\u0bcd\u0ba3\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0b8e\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0bb1\u0bc1 \u0b89\u0ba9\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1 \u0ba4\u0bc6\u0bb0\u0bbf\u0baf\u0bbe\u0ba4\u0bc1. \u0b85\u0bae\u0bcd\u0bae\u0bbe\u0bb5\u0bc8 \u0bb5\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc1 \u0b9a\u0bc2\u0bae\u0bcd\u0baa\u0bbf\u0bb2\u0bcd \u0b89\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbe\u0bb0 \u0bb5\u0bc8\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bc1 \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0bb5\u0bc7\u0bb2\u0bc8 \u0b9a\u0bc6\u0baf\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd. \u0b85\u0ba4\u0bc1 \u0b8e\u0bb5\u0bcd\u0bb5\u0bb3\u0bb5\u0bc1 \u0ba8\u0bc7\u0bb0\u0bae\u0bcd \u0ba4\u0bbe\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0b9a\u0bcd\u0b9a\u0bbf\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9\u0bc1 \u0ba8\u0bbf\u0ba9\u0bc8\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd? \u0b92\u0bb0\u0bc1 \u0bae\u0ba3\u0bbf \u0ba8\u0bc7\u0bb0\u0bae\u0bcd \u0bb5\u0bc7\u0bb2\u0bc8 \u0b9a\u0bc6\u0baf\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd. \u0baa\u0bbf\u0bb0\u0b9a\u0bcd\u0b9a\u0bbf\u0ba9\u0bc8 \u0b8e\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9? \u0b85\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95 \u0b85\u0bae\u0bcd\u0bae\u0bbe \u0bb5\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc1 \u0b9a\u0bc2\u0bae\u0bcd\u0baa\u0bb0\u0bbf\u0bb2\u0bcd \u0b9a\u0bc6\u0baf\u0bcd\u0baf \u0bae\u0bc1\u0b9f\u0bbf\u0baf\u0bb5\u0bbf\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bc8. \u0b85\u0bb5\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0bb5\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc1 \u0b85\u0bb5\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0baa\u0bbf\u0bb0\u0b9a\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bcd, \u0b85\u0bb5\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0b89\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbe\u0bb0\u0bb5\u0bbf\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bc8. \u0b85\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0bb2 \u0baa\u0bcb\u0baf\u0bbf\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd. \u0b87\u0ba4\u0bc7 \u0ba4\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bbe \u0b9a\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bcb\u0bb7 \u0bae\u0bbe\u0bae\u0bbe\u0bb5\u0bbf\u0b9f\u0bae\u0bcd \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0baa\u0ba3\u0bcd\u0ba3\u0bbf\u0baa\u0bcd \u0baa\u0bbe\u0bb0\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd. \u0b85\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0b95\u0bcd \u0b95\u0bb0\u0bcd\u0bae\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1 \u0ba4\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0b92\u0bb5\u0bcd\u0bb5\u0bca\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95 \u0bb5\u0bc0\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1\u0baa\u0bcd \u0baa\u0bcb\u0baf\u0bcd \u0baa\u0bbf\u0bb0\u0b9a\u0bcd\u0b9a\u0bbf\u0ba9\u0bc8 \u0b9a\u0bc6\u0baf\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd. \u0b92\u0bb5\u0bcd\u0bb5\u0bca\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95 \u0bb5\u0bc0\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1 \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0b87\u0bb0\u0ba3\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bc1 \u0bb0\u0bc0\u0bb8\u0ba9\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1 \u0baa\u0bcb\u0ba9\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd. \u0b92\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9\u0bc1 \u0b87\u0ba4\u0bc8\u0baa\u0bcd \u0baa\u0bbe\u0b95\u0bc1\u0bae\u0bcd, \u0b87\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bc8\u0baf\u0bc6\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9\u0bbe... See, \u0b8e\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1 \u0b87\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4 period \u0ba8\u0b9f\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0bb2 \u0bb5\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bb2\u0bcd \u0b87\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bbe\u0bae\u0bcd, \u0b87\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1. But I'm very happy about it. \u0ba8\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0bc8\u0baf \u0bb5\u0bbf\u0bb7\u0baf\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bb3\u0bcd \u0baa\u0b9f\u0bbf\u0b9a\u0bcd\u0b9a\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd. \u0baf\u0bbe\u0bb0\u0bc8\u0baa\u0bcd \u0baa\u0bb1\u0bcd\u0bb1\u0bbf\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9\u0bbe \u0b8e\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9\u0bc8\u0baa\u0bcd \u0baa\u0bb1\u0bcd\u0bb1\u0bbf \u0baa\u0b9f\u0bbf\u0b9a\u0bcd\u0b9a\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd. \u0b87\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9\u0bc1\u0bae\u0bcd \u0baa\u0b9f\u0bbf\u0b9a\u0bcd\u0b9a\u0bc1\u0b9f\u0bc1\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0b87\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd. # 🔴 Why You're Still Getting:


This usually means:

### The OGG codec is unsupported

OGG is a container.

Inside it can be:

- Opus ✅ (works)
- Vorbis ✅ (works)
- Speex ❌ (sometimes fails)
- WhatsApp proprietary variant ❌ (often fails)

Many WhatsApp OGG exports use a weird Opus profile that some APIs reject.

---

# 🔍 Let's Confirm Codec

```

```


If it says something unusual → that’s the problem.

---

# ✅ Easiest Fix (100% Works)

Convert OGG → standard MP3 at low bitrate.

Run:

```
ffmpeg -i 20260219_Sharani_call_2ndaudio.ogg -ac 1 -ar 16000 -b:a 32k fixed_output.mp3
```

Explanation:
- `-ac 1` → mono
- `-ar 16000` → 16 kHz (perfect for speech)
- `-b:a 32k` → very small size
- 1 hour ≈ 14 MB

That will be:
- Under 25 MB
- Clean format
- Fully compatible

Then change script to:

```python
with open("fixed_output.mp3", "rb") as f:
```

---

# 🔵 Why Smaller MP3 Works Better Than WhatsApp OGG

Because:
- It standardizes the codec
- Removes container weirdness
- Converts to pure speech-optimized format

\u0907\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9\u0bbe \u0b9a\u0bca\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bbf\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd. \u0bb4\u0b95\u0baa\u0bcb\u0baf\u0bcd\u0baa\u0bcd\u0baa\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9\u0bbe \u0ba4\u0bc6\u0bb0\u0bbf\u0baf\u0bc1\u0bae\u0bbe, \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bc7 \u0b87\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bc8, \u0ba8\u0bc0\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0b85\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0b95\u0bcd \u0b95\u0bbe\u0bb2\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0bb2 \u0b9a\u0bca\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bbf\u0baa\u0bc7\u0b9a\u0bbf\u0baf\u0bbf\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0baa\u0bcd\u0baa\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd, \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0bae\u0bc1\u0b9f\u0bbf\u0baf\u0bbe\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9\u0bbe, \u0b85\u0baa\u0bcd\u0baa\u0b9f\u0bbf\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0ba9\u0bc1 \u0b9a\u0bca\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bbf\u0baf\u0bbf\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0baa\u0bcd\u0baa\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd, \u0b85\u0bb5\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bb3\u0bc8 \u0b85\u0bb5\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bb3\u0bc8 \u0b85\u0bb5\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bb3\u0bc8 \u0b85\u0bb5\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bb3\u0bc8 \u0baa\u0bc0\u0b9f\u0bcd \u0baa\u0ba3\u0bcd\u0ba3\u0bbf\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0ba4\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd, \u0b87\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bc8\u0baf\u0bbe? \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bc7 \u0b87\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc7 \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bc7 \u0baf\u0bbe\u0bb0\u0bc1 \u0baa\u0bc8\u0b9a\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd? \u0b87\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bc8\u0baf\u0bbe? \u0ba8\u0bbe \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0b8e\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc7 \u0bb5\u0bbe\u0b9a\u0bbf \u0baa\u0bc7\u0b9a\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd? \u0b87\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bc8\u0baf\u0bbe? \u0ba8\u0bbe \u0ba8\u0bbe \u0b8e\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc7 \u0bb5\u0bbe\u0b9a\u0bbf \u0baa\u0bc7\u0b9a\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd?  \u0b87\u0bb5\u0bcd\u0bb5\u0bb3\u0bb5\u0bc1 \u0bae\u0bbe\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd?  \u0ba8\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bbe \u0baa\u0bc7\u0b9a\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd?  \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0b95\u0bca\u0b9f\u0bc1\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bca\u0ba3\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bc1 \u0b87\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0bc7\u0ba9\u0bcd, \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0b87\u0ba4\u0bc8\u0baa\u0bcd \u0baa\u0bb1\u0bcd\u0bb1\u0bbf\u0baf\u0bae\u0bbe\u0b95\u0b9a\u0bcd \u0b9a\u0bca\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bb5\u0bbf\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bc8, \u0ba8\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd superiorly complex, inferiorly complex \u0b8e\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0bb1\u0bc1 \u0b9a\u0bca\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bb2\u0bbe\u0bae\u0bbe?  \u0b85\u0ba4\u0bc1 \u0bb5\u0bb0\u0bbe\u0ba4\u0bc1 \u0bb5\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bbe\u0bb2\u0bcd, \u0b87\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4 concept \u0b87\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0ba4\u0bc1, \u0baa\u0bc1\u0bb0\u0bbf\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0ba4\u0bbe \u0b89\u0ba9\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1? \u0b87\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4 concept \u0b87\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0baa\u0bcd\u0baa\u0bcb\u0ba4\u0bc1 \u0b89\u0ba9\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1 \u0b85\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0baa\u0bcd \u0baa\u0bbf\u0bb0\u0b9a\u0bcd\u0b9a\u0bbf\u0ba9\u0bc8 \u0b87\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0ba4\u0bc1, \u0baa\u0bbf\u0bb0\u0b9a\u0bcd\u0b9a\u0bbf\u0ba9\u0bc8 \u0b8e\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc7 \u0bb5\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0bae\u0bcd \u0b8e\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0bb1\u0bc1 \u0baa\u0bbe\u0bb0\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bbe\u0baf\u0bbf\u0ba9\u0bcd, \u0ba8\u0bae\u0bcd\u0bae \u0b87\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0ba4\u0bbf\u0bb2\u0bc7\u0baf\u0bc7 \u0b95\u0bb7\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bae\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0ba4\u0bbf\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95 \u0b85\u0ba4\u0bbf\u0bb2\u0bc7\u0baf\u0bc7 \u0bae\u0bca\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bbf \u0bae\u0bcb\u0ba4\u0bbf\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0bcb\u0bae\u0bcd. \u0b87\u0baa\u0bcd\u0baa\u0b9f\u0bbf \u0b8e\u0b9f\u0bc1\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bca\u0bb3\u0bcd, \u0b8e\u0ba9\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1 \u0ba4\u0bae\u0bbf\u0bb4\u0bcd, \u0b87\u0b99\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb2\u0bbf\u0bb7\u0bcd \u0ba4\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0bb5\u0bb0\u0bbe\u0ba4\u0bc1, \u0b8e\u0ba9\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1 speciality \u0ba4\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0bae\u0bbe\u0b95\u0bcd\u0bb8\u0bcd, \u0bae\u0bbe\u0b95\u0bcd\u0bb8\u0bbf\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1 \u0baa\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0b9f\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0bb0\u0bbf\u0bb2\u0bc0\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bbe\u0bb0\u0bcd\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0ba4\u0bc1 \u0b87\u0ba9\u0bcd\u0baa\u0bbe\u0bb0\u0bcd \u0baa\u0bbf\u0b9a\u0bbf\u0b95\u0bcd\u0bb8\u0bcd, \u0baa\u0bbf\u0b9a\u0bbf\u0b95\u0bcd\u0bb8\u0bbf\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1 \u0baa\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0b9f\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0bb0\u0bbf\u0bb2\u0bc0\u0b9f\u0bcd\u0b9f\u0bbe\u0bb0\u0bcd\u0ba8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0ba4\u0bc1 \u0b95\u0bc6\u0bae\u0bbf\u0bb8\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bbf\u0bb0\u0bbf, \u0b87\u0ba4\u0bc6\u0bb2\u0bcd\u0bb2\u0bbe\u0bae\u0bcd \u0b93\u0b9f \u0bae\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0baf\u0bae\u0bbe\u0ba9\u0ba4\u0bc1 \u0bae\u0bbe\u0b95\u0bcd\u0bb8\u0bbf\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bc1 \u0baa\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0ba4\u0bcd\u0ba4\u0bc1\u0b9f\u0ba9\u0bcd \u0b87\u0bb0\u0bc1\u0b95\u0bcd\u0b95\u0bbf\u0bb1\u0ba4\u0bc1 # 🔎 First — Your Dashboard Question

From your screenshot:

You see:

```
Responses and Chat Completions → 6 requests
Audio Transcriptions → 0
```

### Why?

Because you are using:

```python
client.audio.transcriptions.create(...)
```

In the new SDK, this is billed under **Responses API**, not legacy Whisper.

So:

✔ It shows under "Responses"  
✔ It will NOT show under "Audio Transcriptions"  
✔ That section is for legacy whisper endpoints  

Your usage is correct.

---

# 🔤 Why Unicode Looks Weird

Your output shows:

```
\u0b85\u0bb5...
```

That is JSON escaped Unicode.

To print Tamil properly:

Instead of:

```python
print(json.dumps(transcript.model_dump(), indent=2))
```

Use:

```python
print(transcript.text)
```

Or:

```python
print(json.dumps(transcript.model_dump(), indent=2, ensure_ascii=False))
```

# 🏢 ENTERPRISE GRADE TRANSCRIPTION PIPELINE


```python
"""
Enterprise Grade Audio Transcription Pipeline
Author: Production Ready Version
Features:
- OGG → MP3 conversion
- Automatic duration detection
- Smart chunking (under OpenAI limits)
- Unicode safe output
- Logging
- Consolidated transcript file
"""

import os
import subprocess
import math
from openai import OpenAI

# =========================
# CONFIGURATION
# =========================

API_KEY = "YOUR_API_KEY"
INPUT_AUDIO = "input_audio.ogg"
WORKING_MP3 = "working_audio.mp3"
CHUNK_FOLDER = "chunks"
FINAL_OUTPUT = "final_transcript.txt"

# OpenAI limits
MAX_DURATION_SECONDS = 1300  # Keep safely below 1400
MODEL_NAME = "gpt-4o-transcribe"

client = OpenAI(api_key=API_KEY)

# =========================
# UTILITY FUNCTIONS
# =========================

def run_command(command):
    """Run shell command safely."""
    result = subprocess.run(command, shell=True)
    if result.returncode != 0:
        raise Exception("Command failed: " + command)

def convert_to_mp3(input_file, output_file):
    """Convert OGG to MP3."""
    print("🔄 Converting to MP3...")
    command = f'ffmpeg -y -i "{input_file}" -ac 1 -ar 16000 "{output_file}"'
    run_command(command)
    print("✅ Conversion complete.")

def get_audio_duration(file_path):
    """Get audio duration in seconds using ffprobe."""
    command = f'ffprobe -i "{file_path}" -show_entries format=duration -v quiet -of csv="p=0"'
    result = subprocess.check_output(command, shell=True)
    return float(result.decode().strip())

def split_audio(file_path, chunk_duration):
    """Split audio into safe chunks."""
    print("✂ Splitting audio into chunks...")
    
    if not os.path.exists(CHUNK_FOLDER):
        os.makedirs(CHUNK_FOLDER)

    total_duration = get_audio_duration(file_path)
    total_chunks = math.ceil(total_duration / chunk_duration)

    print(f"Total duration: {total_duration:.2f} seconds")
    print(f"Creating {total_chunks} chunks...")

    chunk_files = []

    for i in range(total_chunks):
        start_time = i * chunk_duration
        chunk_name = os.path.join(CHUNK_FOLDER, f"chunk_{i+1}.mp3")

        command = (
            f'ffmpeg -y -i "{file_path}" -ss {start_time} '
            f'-t {chunk_duration} "{chunk_name}"'
        )

        run_command(command)
        chunk_files.append(chunk_name)

    print("✅ Splitting complete.")
    return chunk_files

def transcribe_chunk(file_path):
    """Transcribe a single audio chunk."""
    print(f"🎧 Transcribing {file_path}...")
    with open(file_path, "rb") as f:
        transcript = client.audio.transcriptions.create(
            model=MODEL_NAME,
            file=f
        )
    return transcript.text

def save_final_output(full_text):
    """Save consolidated transcript."""
    with open(FINAL_OUTPUT, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write(full_text)
    print(f"📄 Final transcript saved to {FINAL_OUTPUT}")

# =========================
# MAIN EXECUTION
# =========================

def main():
    print("🚀 Starting Enterprise Transcription Pipeline")

    # Step 1: Convert
    convert_to_mp3(INPUT_AUDIO, WORKING_MP3)

    # Step 2: Split
    chunks = split_audio(WORKING_MP3, MAX_DURATION_SECONDS)

    # Step 3: Transcribe
    full_transcript = ""
    for idx, chunk in enumerate(chunks):
        text = transcribe_chunk(chunk)
        full_transcript += f"\n\n--- CHUNK {idx+1} ---\n\n"
        full_transcript += text

    # Step 4: Save
    save_final_output(full_transcript)

    print("✅ Transcription Complete!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

---

You give:

```
input_audio.ogg
```

It will:

✔ Convert to 16k mono MP3  
✔ Detect duration  
✔ Split under 1300 seconds  
✔ Transcribe each safely

### 0182 · CHATGPT · 2026-02-20T23:31:50Z

Senthil — you *did* everything right. Two separate issues hit you, and then a third “why is output tiny?” effect.

## 1) What those errors actually mean

### A) `FileNotFoundError ... .mp3.mp3`
You initially tried to open a filename that doesn’t exist (double `.mp3.mp3`). Python is correct here.

### B) `Audio file might be corrupted or unsupported` (400)
This one is **not** “your file is broken” most of the time. It usually means:

- **Unsupported format for the *transcriptions* endpoint** (OGG is the big one).
- Or the container/codec combination isn’t accepted.
- Or the uploaded file exceeds the endpoint limit (also common).

Per OpenAI docs, **transcriptions file uploads are limited to 25MB** and supported input types for transcriptions are: `mp3, mp4, mpeg, mpga, m4a, wav, webm` — notably **no `ogg`**. citeturn5search1

So: **Yes, your 9MB `.ogg` can still fail** because *format not accepted for transcriptions*. That explains your `.ogg` attempts.

### C) `audio duration 1800.072 seconds is longer than 1400 seconds which is the maximum for this model`
This is a **model duration cap**. You cut 30 minutes (= 1800s) and the model max is 1400s, so it refuses. You proved the fix by cutting to 20 minutes (= 1200s) and it worked.

### D) Why did one 19–20 minute chunk return only 2 lines?
That can happen when **the model’s voice activity detection decides most of the chunk is silence / non-speech / too low volume**, so it outputs very little. It doesn’t mean the file won’t play in Windows Media Player—players can play low-level audio fine.

**Fix:** normalize audio before sending (loudness normalize), and/or cut smaller (10 min) chunks.

---

## 2) Why OpenAI “Usage” shows Audio Transcriptions = 0 but Requests = 6
Your transcript responses show `usage.type = "tokens"` and `input_tokens.audio_tokens=...` (you pasted that). For some org dashboards, token-billed audio may appear under **Responses/Chat Completions**, not under the “Audio Transcriptions” tile (which often expects **duration** accounting). The API reference explicitly shows usage can be **token-based OR duration-based**. citeturn5search2

So your spend is real; it’s just categorized differently in that UI.

---

## 3) Enterprise-grade pipeline you asked for

### Capabilities you requested
- ✅ Input file as CLI parameter (no hardcode)
- ✅ Auto convert (OGG → supported format)
- ✅ Auto chunk to safe duration (< 1400s)
- ✅ Call API per chunk
- ✅ Consolidate into:
  - **one combined transcript**
  - **JSON with timestamps**
  - **speaker diarization** (speaker labels)

### Important constraint
OpenAI diarization is supported via model **`gpt-4o-transcribe-diarize`** and you must request `response_format="diarized_json"` to receive speaker annotations. citeturn5search1turn5search2

---

## 4) “Unicode not readable” on Windows
Use one (or all) of these:
- **VS Code** to open the output files (best)
- In PowerShell: `chcp 65001` (sets UTF-8 codepage for that session)
- In Python: always write files with `encoding="utf-8"` and use `ensure_ascii=False` when dumping JSON

---

## 5) Production-ready Python script (convert → chunk → diarize → merge)

> Prereqs:
> - `pip install openai`
> - `ffmpeg` available in PATH (you already have it)

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Enterprise-grade transcription pipeline for OpenAI Audio Transcriptions:
- Accepts any input audio file (including .ogg)
- Converts to a supported format for transcriptions
- Normalizes audio loudness (reduces "tiny transcript" risk)
- Chunks audio to stay under model duration limit
- Calls OpenAI transcription API per chunk
- Merges into:
  - combined plain text
  - merged diarized JSON with absolute timestamps
  - optional SRT (basic speaker-aware)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import dataclasses
import json
import math
import os
import shutil
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

from openai import OpenAI


# -----------------------------
# Config / limits (model-specific)
# -----------------------------

# You observed 1400s max from the API error, so we enforce a safety margin.
MODEL = "gpt-4o-transcribe-diarize"
RESPONSE_FORMAT = "diarized_json"

MODEL_MAX_SECONDS = 1400
SAFETY_MARGIN_SECONDS = 30
MAX_CHUNK_SECONDS = MODEL_MAX_SECONDS - SAFETY_MARGIN_SECONDS  # e.g., 1370

SUPPORTED_FOR_TRANSCRIPTIONS = {".mp3", ".mp4", ".mpeg", ".mpga", ".m4a", ".wav", ".webm"}


# -----------------------------
# Helpers
# -----------------------------

@dataclasses.dataclass
class ChunkInfo:
    index: int
    start_sec: float
    duration_sec: float
    path: Path


def run(cmd: List[str]) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess:
    """Run a subprocess command with good error messages."""
    try:
        return subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
    except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
        msg = (
            f"Command failed:\n  {' '.join(cmd)}\n\n"
            f"STDOUT:\n{e.stdout}\n\nSTDERR:\n{e.stderr}\n"
        )
        raise RuntimeError(msg) from e


def ffprobe_duration_seconds(path: Path) -> float:
    """Return duration in seconds via ffprobe."""
    cmd = [
        "ffprobe", "-v", "error",
        "-show_entries", "format=duration",
        "-of", "default=noprint_wrappers=1:nokey=1",
        str(path),
    ]
    out = run(cmd).stdout.strip()
    if not out:
        raise RuntimeError(f"ffprobe returned empty duration for: {path}")
    return float(out)


def ensure_ffmpeg_available() -> None:
    if shutil.which("ffmpeg") is None or shutil.which("ffprobe") is None:
        raise RuntimeError("ffmpeg/ffprobe not found in PATH. Install ffmpeg and ensure it's in PATH.")


def convert_and_normalize_to_mp3(
    src: Path,
    dst: Path,
    sample_rate: int = 16000,
    bitrate: str = "32k",
    mono: bool = True,
) -> None:
    """
    Convert arbitrary input to MP3 (supported) + normalize loudness.
    Loudness normalization helps prevent VAD from treating speech as silence.
    """
    channels = "1" if mono else "2"

    # loudnorm is a good general normalization filter
    # If you want faster, remove -af loudnorm (but less robust).
    cmd = [
        "ffmpeg", "-y",
        "-i", str(src),
        "-vn",
        "-ac", channels,
        "-ar", str(sample_rate),
        "-b:a", bitrate,
        "-af", "loudnorm=I=-16:TP=-1.5:LRA=11",
        str(dst),
    ]
    run(cmd)


def cut_chunk(src: Path, dst: Path, start_sec: float, dur_sec: float) -> None:
    """Cut a chunk without re-encoding where possible; re-encode if needed."""
    # For simplicity + consistent decoding, we re-encode to mp3 at same settings.
    cmd = [
        "ffmpeg", "-y",
        "-i", str(src),
        "-ss", seconds_to_hhmmss(start_sec),
        "-t", seconds_to_hhmmss(dur_sec),
        "-vn",
        "-ac", "1",
        "-ar", "16000",
        "-b:a", "32k",
        str(dst),
    ]
    run(cmd)


def seconds_to_hhmmss(seconds: float) -> str:
    s = max(0.0, float(seconds))
    h = int(s // 3600)
    m = int((s % 3600) // 60)
    sec = s % 60
    return f"{h:02d}:{m:02d}:{sec:06.3f}"


def write_text(path: Path, content: str) -> None:
    path.write_text(content, encoding="utf-8")


def write_json(path: Path, obj: Any) -> None:
    path.write_text(json.dumps(obj, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")


# -----------------------------
# OpenAI call
# -----------------------------

def transcribe_diarized(client: OpenAI, audio_path: Path, language: Optional[str] = None) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """
    Calls OpenAI audio transcription with diarization.

    Returns diarized JSON (dict).
    """
    with audio_path.open("rb") as f:
        kwargs: Dict[str, Any] = {
            "model": MODEL,
            "file": f,
            "response_format": RESPONSE_FORMAT,  # required for speaker segments
        }
        # Optional: force language if you know it (e.g., "ta" for Tamil, "en" for English).
        # For mixed Tamil/English, leaving it unset often works better.
        if language:
            kwargs["language"] = language

        result = client.audio.transcriptions.create(**kwargs)

    # The SDK returns a pydantic object; convert safely.
    if hasattr(result, "model_dump"):
        return result.model_dump()
    return dict(result)


# -----------------------------
# Merge diarized segments
# -----------------------------

def merge_diarized_chunks(chunks: List[ChunkInfo], results: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """
    Merge diarized_json responses into one timeline with absolute timestamps.
    Expected diarized_json structure includes segments with start/end and speaker labels.
    """
    merged_segments: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
    combined_text_parts: List[str] = []

    for ch, res in zip(chunks, results):
        # Defensive parsing: different snapshots can name fields slightly differently
        text = res.get("text") or res.get("transcript") or ""
        if text:
            combined_text_parts.append(text.strip())

        segs = res.get("segments") or []
        for seg in segs:
            seg2 = dict(seg)
            # Common fields: start, end, speaker, text
            if "start" in seg2:
                seg2["start"] = float(seg2["start"]) + ch.start_sec
            if "end" in seg2:
                seg2["end"] = float(seg2["end"]) + ch.start_sec
            seg2["chunk_index"] = ch.index
            merged_segments.append(seg2)

    merged = {
        "model": MODEL,
        "response_format": RESPONSE_FORMAT,
        "duration_sec": merged_segments[-1]["end"] if merged_segments and "end" in merged_segments[-1] else None,
        "text": "\n\n".join([p for p in combined_text_parts if p]),
        "segments": merged_segments,
    }
    return merged


def diarized_json_to_basic_srt(merged: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    """
    Convert diarized segments into a basic SRT.
    (SRT does not have a native 'speaker' field; we prefix lines with Speaker.)
    """
    segs = merged.get("segments") or []
    lines: List[str] = []
    idx = 1

    for seg in segs:
        start = float(seg.get("start", 0.0))
        end = float(seg.get("end", start + 1.0))
        speaker = seg.get("speaker") or seg.get("speaker_label") or "SPK"
        text = (seg.get("text") or "").strip()
        if not text:
            continue

        lines.append(str(idx))
        lines.append(f"{srt_time(start)} --> {srt_time(end)}")
        lines.append(f"{speaker}: {text}")
        lines.append("")  # blank line
        idx += 1

    return "\n".join(lines)


def srt_time(seconds: float) -> str:
    # SRT format: HH:MM:SS,mmm
    s = max(0.0, float(seconds))
    h = int(s // 3600)
    m = int((s % 3600) // 60)
    sec = int(s % 60)
    ms = int(round((s - math.floor(s)) * 1000))
    return f"{h:02d}:{m:02d}:{sec:02d},{ms:03d}"


# -----------------------------
# Main pipeline
# -----------------------------

def build_chunks(src_mp3: Path, total_sec: float, max_chunk_sec: float) -> List[ChunkInfo]:
    chunks: List[ChunkInfo] = []
    start = 0.0
    idx = 1
    while start < total_sec - 0.1:
        dur = min(max_chunk_sec, total_sec - start)
        chunks.append(ChunkInfo(index=idx, start_sec=start, duration_sec=dur, path=Path()))
        start += dur
        idx += 1
    return chunks


def main() -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description="Enterprise transcription: convert + chunk + diarize + merge (OpenAI Audio Transcriptions)."
    )
    parser.add_argument("input_audio", help="Path to input audio file (ogg/mp3/m4a/wav/etc).")
    parser.add_argument("--outdir", default="out_transcripts", help="Output directory.")
    parser.add_argument("--language", default=None, help="Optional language hint (e.g., ta, en). Leave unset for mixed.")
    parser.add_argument("--chunk-seconds", type=int, default=MAX_CHUNK_SECONDS,
                        help=f"Chunk duration in seconds (must be <= {MAX_CHUNK_SECONDS} recommended).")
    args = parser.parse_args()

    ensure_ffmpeg_available()

    in_path = Path(args.input_audio).expanduser().resolve()
    if not in_path.exists():
        print(f"ERROR: input file not found: {in_path}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 2

    outdir = Path(args.outdir).expanduser().resolve()
    outdir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    # Work in a temp folder for intermediate files
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="oai_transcribe_") as tmpd:
        tmp = Path(tmpd)

        normalized_mp3 = tmp / "normalized.mp3"

        # Always convert+normalize to stable MP3 (even if already mp3) for consistency
        print(f"[1/5] Converting + normalizing to MP3: {normalized_mp3}")
        convert_and_normalize_to_mp3(in_path, normalized_mp3)

        total_sec = ffprobe_duration_seconds(normalized_mp3)
        print(f"[2/5] Duration: {total_sec:.2f}s (~{total_sec/60:.1f} min)")

        chunk_sec = float(args.chunk_seconds)
        if chunk_sec > MAX_CHUNK_SECONDS:
            print(f"WARNING: chunk-seconds {chunk_sec} > safe max {MAX_CHUNK_SECONDS}. Forcing safe max.")
            chunk_sec = float(MAX_CHUNK_SECONDS)

        chunks = build_chunks(normalized_mp3, total_sec, chunk_sec)

        # Cut chunks
        chunk_paths: List[ChunkInfo] = []
        for ch in chunks:
            ch_path = tmp / f"chunk_{ch.index:03d}.mp3"
            print(f"[3/5] Cutting chunk {ch.index}: start={ch.start_sec:.2f}s dur={ch.duration_sec:.2f}s")
            cut_chunk(normalized_mp3, ch_path, ch.start_sec, ch.duration_sec)
            chunk_paths.append(dataclasses.replace(ch, path=ch_path))

        # OpenAI client (expects OPENAI_API_KEY env var or other configured auth)
        client = OpenAI()

        # Transcribe each chunk
        results: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
        for ch in chunk_paths:
            print(f"[4/5] Transcribing chunk {ch.index} (diarization on)...")
            res = transcribe_diarized(client, ch.path, language=args.language)
            results.append(res)

            # Save per-chunk raw response (useful for debugging)
            write_json(outdir / f"chunk_{ch.index:03d}.diarized.json", res)

        # Merge
        merged = merge_diarized_chunks(chunk_paths, results)

        # Outputs
        merged_json_path = outdir / "merged.diarized.json"
        merged_txt_path = outdir / "merged.txt"
        merged_srt_path = outdir / "merged.srt"

        write_json(merged_json_path, merged)
        write_text(merged_txt_path, merged.get("text", ""))

        # Basic SRT from segments
        srt = diarized_json_to_basic_srt(merged)
        write_text(merged_srt_path, srt)

        print("[5/5] Done.")
        print(f"  - {merged_txt_path}")
        print(f"  - {merged_json_path}")
        print(f"  - {merged_srt_path}")

    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
```

### Run it (examples)
```powershell
# UTF-8 console (helps display Tamil)
chcp 65001

# Diarized + timestamped outputs
python .\transcribe_enterprise.py "C:\path\to\20260219_Sharani_call_2ndaudio.ogg" --outdir ".\out"

# If it's mostly Tamil, you can hint language (optional)
python .\transcribe_enterprise.py ".\20260219_Sharani_call_2ndaudio.ogg" --language ta --outdir ".\out"
```

---

## 6) About “automatic speaker diarization accuracy”
Diarization is *best-effort* even with the diarize model:
- It can still confuse speakers in overlaps, similar voices, or noisy lines.
- But it’s **far better than guessing from text** (like “Dau:” labels you saw earlier).

If you want **higher reliability**, the enterprise approach is:
- run **local diarization** (pyannote / whisperx) to get speaker segments,
- then run OpenAI transcription on chunks,
- then align transcript to diarization segments.
That’s heavier, but it’s “bank-grade”.

For now, this script gives you a clean, repeatable, auditable pipeline using the officially supported diarization model. citeturn5search1turn5search2

---

## Why Edge uses so much memory

Modern browsers:
- Isolate each tab in a separate process
- Keep JavaScript engines running
- Cache large page content
- Keep history & render trees in memory
